## Approved Plan:
## Outcome
Create a durable, indexed archive of the full three-day Shera architecture/grilling work — every user decision, recommendation and alternative, benchmark/founder finding, problem and correction, external reference, skill used, and source artifact — while leaving `docs/PRD.md` completely unchanged.

## 1. Preserve the original sources before rewriting summaries
Create a **local-only raw archive** at `.session-archive/2026-09-08-to-2026-09-11/` and git-ignore it so raw Codex JSONL (which may contain machine paths, internal prompts, or sensitive tool payloads) is not accidentally published.

Copy, when present:
- the predecessor Codex rollout containing the five-repository benchmark and Question 1;
- the main Codex rollout containing Decisions 1–38 and Question 39;
- `C:\Users\User\shera_codex_transcript.txt`;
- the full office-hours memo;
- the referenced clipboard image (if it still exists; otherwise record it as a missing source and preserve its transcript description);
- any available raw export for this ZCode continuation.

Generate a SHA-256 source manifest with original path, archive path, size, timestamp, role, extraction status, and any missing-source note. The tracked documentation will contain complete readable transcripts; the raw archive remains local evidence.

## 2. Add a tracked, readable session archive
Create `docs/history/2026-09-08-to-2026-09-11/` with four focused files:

1. **`README.md` — canonical session record**
   - purpose and three-day timeline;
   - source inventory and checksums;
   - exact session/thread IDs, model/provider/CLI data where available;
   - skills used and snapshotted (`grilling`, `grill-me`, `grill-with-docs`, office-hours context), with version/path and what was actually invoked versus merely available;
   - all known obstacles and corrections;
   - current authoritative status and reading order.

2. **`codex-transcript.md` — complete original grilling transcript**
   - combine the predecessor and continued Codex rollouts chronologically;
   - preserve every user and assistant message without summarizing it away;
   - include Question 1 through Question 39, every recommendation/alternative, timestamps, decision confirmations, the unanswered D39 reply, and the final HTTP 402 `Budget pool quota has been exhausted` failure;
   - preserve references to tool/research actions and surface relevant failures, while excluding hidden system/developer prompts and credentials from the tracked copy.

3. **`zcode-continuation-transcript.md` — complete continuation transcript**
   - preserve the current session from “Can you see the last Codex runs?” through Decisions 40–45 and this documentation work;
   - include the exact Q40–Q45 questions, alternatives, recommendations, and user answers;
   - include the user's constraints: one question at a time, Agent everywhere, keep Operations, and never modify the previous PRD;
   - record all documentation actions and corrections rather than only the final state.

4. **`findings-obstacles-references.md` — evidence ledger**
   - preserve the five-repository benchmark findings for OpenHands/Agent Canvas, Agent Zero, LibreChat, Dify, and SuperAGI;
   - preserve the office-hours founder memo in full as an appendix, including the conflict between its “narrow coding wedge first” recommendation and the later choice to design the full platform;
   - list every external URL/reference extracted from the transcript (OpenHands, Agent Zero, LibreChat, Dify, SuperAGI, Drizzle, Prisma, Kysely, skill docs, and the attached image);
   - for each reference record: claim/use, source URL/path, verification level, and any uncertainty/citation mismatch (including the LibreChat/Dify attribution inconsistency);
   - document all known problems/obstacles: AgentRouter 402 quota exhaustion, D39 acknowledgement loss, Explore-agent model-request failures, unavailable current-session raw export if still unavailable, missing attachment if applicable, PRD conflicts, stale index/status contradictions, the initial inappropriate self-decision of D40–D49 and later correction, PRD addendum creation/reversion, monolith-to-split decision docs, and the `Expert`→`Agent` blind-replacement artifacts that were fixed.

## 3. Repair and complete the existing decision records
Update the three files under `docs/decisions/` without touching the PRD:

- **`README.md`**
  - correct the stale headline (“40–49 defaults”) to the real state: D1–D45 plus E1–E8;
  - distinguish confirmed, confirmed-from-explicit-user-reply-with-missing-acknowledgement (D39), provisional (D13/D19), refined, superseded, deferred, and engineering decisions;
  - state clearly that criteria 22–27 were adopted in D45 but are recorded outside the unchanged PRD;
  - link to the full session archive, glossary, traceability matrix, and raw-source manifest.

- **`confirmed-decisions.md`**
  - preserve historical decision text;
  - make D39's evidence status exact: explicit user acceptance, assistant acknowledgement interrupted by HTTP 402;
  - visibly mark provisional/refined/superseded decisions;
  - mark historical “Expert” wording as superseded by D44 instead of leaving it visually normative.

- **`closure-decisions.md`**
  - preserve D40–D45 and E1–E8;
  - include links back to the exact Q40–Q45 transcript evidence;
  - preserve the original P1–P9 draft alternatives/rationale and their final dispositions so the path to the result is not lost;
  - remove every implication that D46–D49 exist.

## 4. Add canonical terminology and reconciliation documents
Create:

- **`docs/GLOSSARY.md`** — authoritative vocabulary: Pack → Operations → Agents; Operation definition vs Operation workspace/chat vs task run; Agent identity/session/worker; workspace vs user container; Skill vs Tool vs Connector; central PostgreSQL vs local SQLite; platform credits vs production billing. Historical `Expert` wording is an alias only and not a separate concept.

- **`docs/TRACEABILITY.md`** — one row for every D1–D45 and E1–E8 containing status, evidence/session, supersedes/superseded-by, conflicting PRD section, implementation boundary/module, and test or acceptance criterion. Include D45 criteria 22–27 as adopted external criteria.

Add an explicit **PRD reconciliation matrix** here: the PRD remains the untouched 2026-09-08 baseline, while later decisions are authoritative for conflicting items. Record the user's explicit instruction not to modify the PRD as the reason this reconciliation lives separately, despite PRD §24's normal update rule.

## 5. Make the archive impossible for future agents to miss
Create/update entry points:

- **root `README.md`** — short project overview and “Start here” documentation links;
- **root `AGENTS.md`** — mandatory reading order for future agents:
  1. `docs/README.md`
  2. unchanged `docs/PRD.md`
  3. `docs/decisions/README.md`
  4. session-history README/transcripts
  5. glossary and traceability matrix

  It will explicitly say: do not modify the PRD without a new direct instruction; later decision records supersede conflicting PRD wording; do not reintroduce `Expert`; preserve decision evidence and status when changing architecture.

- **`docs/README.md`** — canonical documentation index linking PRD, decision records, session archive, glossary, traceability, office-hours findings, transcripts, references, and raw-source manifest.

Cross-link all index/readme files so no important record is reachable only through a machine-local path.

## 6. Verification gate
Run deterministic checks before reporting completion:

- `git diff -- docs/PRD.md` must be empty and the file must remain at its original 338-line baseline;
- validate unique/contiguous D1–D45 and E1–E8 IDs and all status labels;
- confirm no stale “D40–D49 defaults” or “criteria 22–27 proposed” wording remains;
- check Markdown relative links and referenced files;
- compare extracted transcript message counts/timestamps against both Codex rollouts;
- ensure every URL/path extracted from the transcripts appears in the reference/source ledger;
- search canonical docs for stale `Expert` usage, allowing it only in historical transcript/quoted-decision contexts with the D44 note;
- verify all archived source checksums;
- review the final git diff to ensure changes are limited to documentation/index/archive protection files and do not touch the PRD or application code;
- do not commit or push.

## Important preservation boundary
“Nothing lost” will mean every user/assistant discussion, decision path, finding, obstacle, correction, and reference is preserved in tracked, readable Markdown. Exact raw machine rollouts will also be copied locally and checksummed, but kept out of Git to avoid publishing hidden prompts, credentials, or sensitive tool payloads.

<summary>Agent Explore task &quot;Audit current decision records&quot; completed.</summary>
<result>## Scope reviewed

- `E:\PROJECTS\shera-bot\docs\PRD.md`
- `E:\PROJECTS\shera-bot\docs\decisions\README.md`
- `E:\PROJECTS\shera-bot\docs\decisions\confirmed-decisions.md`

All four files are currently untracked in Git. No files were modified.

## Executive assessment

The decision set is unusually detailed and preserves many important implementation constraints, supersession relationships, and the provider-quota obstacle. However, it is not yet a fully consistent source of truth.

The largest issue is that the PRD is explicitly unchanged even though decisions D29–D45 materially redefine the product model, terminology, acceptance criteria, runtime lifecycle, and initial agent catalog. The decision README acknowledges this, but the repository now has two simultaneously authoritative-looking descriptions of the product.

There are also concrete metadata contradictions, missing provenance, lost draft content, and several unresolved implementation boundaries.

---

## 1. Numbering and status problems

### 1.1 README claims decisions 40–49, but only D40–D45 exist

`E:\PROJECTS\shera-bot\docs\decisions\README.md:3` says:

&gt; decisions 1–39 confirmed; 40–49 recorded as recommended closure defaults

But the files contain:

- D1–D39 in `confirmed-decisions.md`
- D40–D45 in `closure-decisions.md`
- E1–E8 engineering decisions
- P1–P9 proposal dispositions

There are no D46–D49 records.

This is the clearest numbering error and should be corrected or explained. If 46–49 were intended future questions, they should be listed as explicitly pending rather than described as recorded decisions.

### 1.2 D39 is not equivalent in evidentiary status to D1–D38

`confirmed-decisions.md:4` says all decisions are confirmed except D39, which was recorded from an unanswered reply. D39 itself repeats this at lines 335–339.

D39 is therefore not strictly “confirmed by the product owner.” It is an inference from the last user message before the quota failure:

&gt; “for the prototype yes”

The current status label, `Confirmed — recorded from the previously unanswered reply`, is internally understandable but does not fit the README’s simpler “Confirmed” definition.

Recommended status vocabulary should distinguish at least:

- Confirmed
- Confirmed from explicit answer
- Inferred from incomplete session
- Confirmed but refined
- Provisional
- Superseded
- Engineering decision
- Deferred
- Open/unresolved

D39 should be marked something like **Inferred from incomplete session — requires explicit reconfirmation**, unless the product owner has separately reconfirmed it.

### 1.3 D45 status conflicts with the README

`README.md:60-67` labels acceptance criteria 22–27 as:

&gt; Proposed acceptance criteria additions (not yet applied to the PRD)

But `closure-decisions.md:48-56` says D45 confirmed them and `closure-decisions.md:95` calls them “adopted in D45.”

Both can be technically true if “confirmed” means decided but “not yet applied” means not copied into the PRD. The distinction is not stated clearly enough.

The documentation should explicitly separate:

- **Decision status:** confirmed
- **Specification synchronization status:** not yet incorporated into PRD
- **Acceptance status:** not yet implemented or verified

### 1.4 D13 and D19 are simultaneously confirmed and provisional

The following records use mixed status semantics:

- D13: `Confirmed (provisional for the prototype)`
- D19: `Confirmed (provisional; refined by D29)`

This is not necessarily wrong, but the files do not define whether “provisional” means:

- chosen for the prototype but expected to be revisited,
- not yet technically validated,
- subject to a spike,
- or merely lower confidence.

D13 explicitly requires a process-supervision spike, but there is no linked result or acceptance test proving when the provisional status can be closed.

### 1.5 E1–E8 have no uniform status labels

D40–D45 have explicit status lines. E1–E8 do not. The README calls them “engineering decisions,” but there is no consistent metadata for:

- owner,
- date,
- authority,
- whether binding,
- superseded-by relationship,
- implementation evidence,
- review trigger.

They should be included in the same decision index as D1–D45, with an explicit `Engineering decision` status.

---

## 2. PRD and decision-record inconsistencies

### 2.1 Dashboard and chat taxonomy conflict

The PRD says:

- `PRD.md:79-81`: sidebar contains persistent Researcher, Developer, and General Assistant chats, task chats, and group chats.
- `PRD.md:83-91`: defines Agent chat, Task-specific chat, and Multi-agent group chat.

D29–D31 and D41–D44 redefine this as:

- exactly two sidebar sections: Agents and Operations,
- persistent direct Agent chats,
- reusable Operation workspaces,
- independently tracked task runs inside an Operation,
- ad-hoc Operations,
- no separate task-specific chat type.

Relevant records:

- `closure-decisions.md:16-22` (D41)
- `confirmed-decisions.md:212-251` (D29–D31)
- `closure-decisions.md:41-46` (D44)

The README summarizes the change but does not provide a complete replacement specification. An implementer reading the PRD could build the retired three-chat-type model.

### 2.2 Three agents versus six Agents

The PRD states:

- `PRD.md:20`: validate three independent agents.
- `PRD.md:103`: all agents are separate Pi processes with fixed roles.
- `PRD.md:105-129`: only Researcher, Developer, and General Assistant are specified.
- `PRD.md:247-248`: acceptance requires three agent chats and three Pi processes.

D43 changes the first pack to six Agents:

- General Assistant
- Researcher
- Developer
- Citation Manager
- Programming Tutor
- Study Planner

It also adds three pack-defined Operations:

- Research Paper
- Programming Assignment
- Exam Preparation

This is recorded at `closure-decisions.md:32-39`, but the PRD remains unchanged. The acceptance criteria therefore still test the old three-agent model.

### 2.3 Pi lifecycle conflict

The PRD says:

- `PRD.md:133`: each user container contains three Pi processes.
- `PRD.md:201`: all Pi processes restart during recovery.

D32 instead defines three separate lifecycles:

- durable Agent identity,
- on-demand warm Agent session,
- persistent task worker governed by task state.

See `confirmed-decisions.md:253-261`.

The implementation consequence is substantial: the system no longer has three permanently running role processes. The PRD needs an explicit lifecycle correction or a clearly authoritative decision overlay.

### 2.4 Central PostgreSQL versus local SQLite authority

The PRD says:

- `PRD.md:139`: cloud deployment includes PostgreSQL and Redis.
- `PRD.md:227`: PostgreSQL stores central metadata, durable task routing, and operational state.
- `PRD.md:233`: TypeScript adapter around the Pi fork.

D2, D8, D10, and D11 establish a more specific split:

- central PostgreSQL owns control-plane records only,
- product state is local to each user container,
- SQLite is the local authoritative store,
- one runtime gateway owns SQLite writes and authoritative transitions,
- Redis is not the source of truth.

See:

- `confirmed-decisions.md:14-22`
- `confirmed-decisions.md:47-75`

The README’s reconciliation note is helpful, but it does not enumerate every PRD sentence that must be reinterpreted. The phrases “durable task routing” and “operational state” in PRD §17 remain broad enough to contradict D2/D10.

### 2.5 Architecture decisions supersede the PRD’s “engineering choice” boundary

The PRD says at `PRD.md:235` that low-level library versions, cloud provider, database table names, module boundaries, and visual details remain engineering decisions.

D23–D25 then make specific engineering choices:

- three deployable apps,
- shared contract packages,
- feature modules,
- Drizzle,
- separate PostgreSQL and SQLite schemas/migrations,
- Zod-first contracts,
- generated OpenAPI and clients.

See `confirmed-decisions.md:137-185`.

These decisions may legitimately refine the boundary, but the documentation should say which choices are now fixed and which remain open. Otherwise the PRD still suggests engineering can choose module boundaries and ORM freely.

### 2.6 Acceptance criteria 22–27 are missing from PRD §19

The PRD has criteria 1–21 at `PRD.md:243-265`.

D45 adds criteria 22–27 at `closure-decisions.md:48-56`:

- version-pinned customer pack and task snapshots,
- Operation launch preview,
- multiple independently tracked task runs,
- browser-close/restart survival,
- mutation previews and reusable grants,
- explicit private-memory publication.

The README calls these “proposed,” while D45 calls them adopted. They must be represented in one canonical acceptance register even if the PRD itself is intentionally not edited.

### 2.7 PRD TODO registers are stale

PRD §21 and §22 (`PRD.md:284-330`) still contain TODOs that decisions have partially resolved.

Examples:

- Visual navigation is no longer open at a conceptual level: D41/D44 define Agents and Operations.
- Pi adapter shape is substantially defined by D13/D14.
- Repository/module strategy is defined by D23.
- ORM and migration strategy is defined by D24.
- Contract strategy is defined by D25.
- approval schema and reusable grants are defined by D9/D35.
- event cursors and replay are defined by D5/D26.
- memory provenance and scopes are defined by D37.
- build order and testing defaults are defined by D23/E7/E8.

The TODO register needs dispositions such as:

- Resolved by D#
- Partially resolved; implementation detail remains
- Still open
- Deferred
- Replaced by later decision

### 2.8 Prototype scope and admin console conflict

E7 says:

&gt; Phase 7 (remote/cloud backends, admin console, benchmark trend tracking) is optional for the private prototype.

But the PRD acceptance criteria require:

- automatic backups,
- administrator restore,
- administrator capabilities in §15,
- a Balanced-tier container provisioned through the product.

See `PRD.md:213-215` and `PRD.md:260-263`.

If the admin console is optional, the documentation must distinguish:

- backend/admin API capability required for acceptance,
- full admin UI optional,
- manual/operator procedure acceptable for the prototype.

Otherwise E7 appears to weaken existing acceptance criteria.

---

## 3. Terminology inconsistencies

### 3.1 “Expert” versus “Agent”

D44 mandates “Agent everywhere”:

- `closure-decisions.md:41-46`

However, `confirmed-decisions.md` contains normative-looking “Expert” terminology throughout D29–D43, including:

- D29 title and definitions,
- D32 lifecycle description,
- D36 roster behavior,
- D37 private memory,
- D38 capability intersection,
- D39 identity model.

D29 also explicitly says:

&gt; “Expert” is the user-facing term; “agent” is the technical term

at `confirmed-decisions.md:234`, which D44 directly supersedes.

Historical wording should be preserved, but the records need stronger visual marking, for example:

- “Historical terminology; superseded by D44”
- canonical term: Agent
- historical alias: Expert
- do not use in new UI/schema/code

The PRD still uses “agent,” which is good, but does not define the new Pack → Operations → Agents model.

### 3.2 “Scene” is mentioned only as a historical rename

D29 says Scene was renamed Operation. There is no explicit alias table covering:

- Scene → Operation
- Expert → Agent
- group chat → Operation workspace
- task-specific chat → task run within direct Agent chat or Operation
- three fixed agents → pack-defined Agent catalog

A canonical glossary is needed.

### 3.3 Workspace and container are different concepts but underdefined

D40 says:

- one workspace per user,
- the container is the workspace,
- all records are internally scoped by `workspace_id`.

The PRD uses “workspace” for files, projects, shared state, and user environment, but does not define whether this is:

- a domain object,
- a storage boundary,
- a UI concept,
- a container,
- or all four.

The documentation should explicitly define:

- user,
- workspace,
- container,
- Operation workspace/chat,
- project workspace,
- task scope,
- Agent-private folder.

### 3.4 Skill, Tool, and Connector terminology is absent from the PRD

D38 provides precise definitions and a layered capability intersection at `confirmed-decisions.md:315-333`, but PRD §7 uses tools and integrations without adopting those distinctions.

This is a security-relevant documentation gap because implementation teams could accidentally make a Skill executable or allow a Connector to authorize itself.

---

## 4. Cross-link and provenance findings

### 4.1 Relative internal links appear valid

The following links are structurally sound:

- `README.md` → `../PRD.md`
- `README.md` → sibling decision files
- `confirmed-decisions.md` → `README.md`
- `closure-decisions.md` → `README.md`
- `closure-decisions.md` → `confirmed-decisions.md`

No broken internal Markdown links were found in the reviewed files.

### 4.2 The PRD has no links back to decisions

The decision README has a PRD touchpoint table, but the PRD has no decision IDs or links back to the authoritative refinements.

For example, a reader of `PRD.md:79-91` receives no indication that D41 supersedes the chat taxonomy. A reader of `PRD.md:103-129` receives no indication that D43 expands the first pack to six Agents.

At minimum, the repository needs a reconciliation matrix linked from the PRD or decision README.

### 4.3 Provenance relies on machine-local paths

`README.md:69-74` references:

- `~/.codex/sessions/...`
- `C:\Users\User\.gstack\projects\shera-bot\user-main-design-20260908-134815.md`

These are not portable repository references. The files currently exist on this machine, but another contributor or CI environment will not have them.

One provenance path is also truncated:

&gt; `rollout-2026-09-08T13-37-36-01a07ff3-...jsonl`

That cannot uniquely identify a durable source.

### 4.4 Continued-session provenance is missing

The README gives detailed provenance for the original run and quota failure, but D40–D45 have no session ID, transcript path, or message-level source reference.

Each of D40–D45 includes a short answer excerpt such as:

- “yes go with this”
- “option 1”
- “option 2”
- “ok”
- “Agent everywhere…”

But there is no durable record of:

- the exact question,
- the alternatives presented,
- the full answer,
- the timestamp,
- the thread/session ID,
- whether the answer was edited or interpreted.

### 4.5 Important findings are named but not preserved

The README mentions:

- a five-repository benchmark,
- an office-hours founder assessment,
- a design memo concluding the vision was unvalidated and too broad for a solo founder.

However, the decision files do not preserve the actual findings, comparison results, citations, or durable repository links.

The benchmark repositories named are:

- OpenHands
- Agent Zero
- LibreChat
- Dify
- SuperAGI

Those findings should be preserved as a dated research artifact, even if they are not product decisions.

### 4.6 The obstacle is preserved, but not operationally classified

The provider quota failure is well documented at:

- `README.md:71`
- `confirmed-decisions.md:336-339`

It includes the HTTP 402 condition and the fact that no assistant reply was produced. This is good.

What is missing is a clear classification:

- obstacle caused incomplete confirmation of D39,
- D39 is not equivalent to all other confirmed decisions,
- continued session was a recovery procedure,
- the resumed decisions require separate provenance.

---

## 5. Missing information in the decision records

### 5.1 Draft proposal content is not preserved

`closure-decisions.md:60-74` gives only the P1–P9 titles and dispositions.

The file says the “Draft proposals” section contains assistant recommendations, but the actual proposals are absent. This means the repository does not preserve:

- the original recommendation,
- alternatives considered,
- rationale,
- objections,
- why the proposal was accepted, rejected, or delegated,
- what changed between draft and final decision.

This directly conflicts with the requested preservation of every decision, finding, obstacle, and reference.

### 5.2 No complete decision index

There is no single table containing:

- ID,
- title,
- status,
- date,
- decision owner,
- evidence source,
- supersedes,
- superseded by,
- related PRD sections,
- implementation status,
- test/acceptance coverage.

The narrative files require manual parsing and make it easy for the README, decision records, and PRD to drift.

### 5.3 No explicit unresolved register

The closure file says:

&gt; The frontier is now empty.

That is too broad. Several implementation questions remain materially unspecified or only partially resolved, including:

- exact admin-console/UI scope versus backend-only administration,
- internal credits versus production billing/metering,
- custody and backup treatment for admin-funded provider credentials,
- whether provider credentials are ever inside the user container,
- exact event compaction policy and limits,
- exact local database backup/restore consistency procedure,
- `workspace_id` invariants and future multi-workspace migration,
- pack manifest and `operation.yaml` schemas,
- mapping of the six Agents to the three Operations,
- coordinator lifecycle and behavior when removed,
- exact recovery semantics for each tool class,
- whether Phase 7 is genuinely outside acceptance or only outside the build order.

These should be marked open, delegated, or explicitly “implementation-defined.”

### 5.4 No complete acceptance traceability

The decision records define D45 criteria 22–27 but do not map each criterion to:

- implementation module,
- contract/schema,
- end-to-end test,
- manual QA procedure,
- evidence artifact,
- owner.

The same is true for many existing PRD criteria.

### 5.5 No explicit decision-to-implementation mapping

D23 provides a proposed repository tree and many maintainability rules, but the records do not map decisions such as D9, D26, D32, D35, D37, or D38 to concrete modules, tables, protocols, or test suites.

### 5.6 No decision change protocol

PRD §24 says changes require an explicit product decision and PRD update. The decision files do not define:

- how a new decision is numbered,
- how a decision is amended,
- how supersession is recorded,
- how the PRD synchronization is tracked,
- whether implementation can proceed on a provisional decision,
- who can approve a product versus engineering decision.

---

## 6. Specific terminology and architecture gaps that should be resolved

### Provider credentials and credits

The PRD says:

- provider keys stay inside the user container (`PRD.md:171`);
- central service does not persist provider keys;
- provider credits and billing are future work in some contexts.

D17, D20, and D22 add:

- admin-funded providers,
- internal credit reservations and settlement,
- deployment-level admin credentials,
- optional user provider credentials,
- no mock fallback.

D22 says admin-funded credentials are deployment secrets and should not be written to SQLite, while D17 says provider credentials stay inside the user container. The intended exception should be written explicitly.

The documentation should define:

- admin credential location,
- user credential location,
- whether either is included in backups,
- how provider identity is represented in task snapshots,
- how “credits” differ from billing or real payments,
- whether credit exhaustion is a user-visible task pause state.

### Acceptance and admin scope

E7’s statement that the admin console is optional needs reconciliation with the PRD’s admin and backup acceptance criteria.

### Initial Agent/Operation mapping

D43 says each Agent is reused across at least two Operations, but does not give the full mapping. The examples only mention Researcher. The pack manifest needs a concrete matrix.

### Operation object model

D31 distinguishes:

1. Operation definition
2. Operation workspace/chat
3. Operation task/run

D41 adds pack-defined and ad-hoc Operations. D40 adds the user workspace. The hierarchy and IDs should be documented explicitly.

### Recovery and task states

D8 lists task states, while D32 introduces `WAITING_FOR_TOOL` and recovery display statuses `RECOVERING`, `RESUMED`, and `RECOVERY_REQUIRED`. `WAITING_FOR_TOOL` is not present in the D8 state list, and the recovery display statuses are not clearly distinguished from persisted task states.

The records should separate:

- persisted task state,
- process state,
- recovery mode,
- UI status,
- terminal/non-terminal state.

---

## Recommended implementation plan for preserving all decisions and evidence

### Phase 1: Establish a canonical decision registry

Create one authoritative index covering:

- D1–D45
- E1–E8
- P1–P9 and their full dispositions
- deferred items
- unresolved items

Each record should have structured metadata:

- ID
- title
- category: product, engineering, proposal, deferred, unresolved
- status
- date
- owner/authority
- source session and message
- rationale
- alternatives
- consequences
- supersedes
- superseded by
- related PRD sections
- related acceptance criteria
- implementation status
- test/evidence references

Fix the README range to D40–D45 unless D46–D49 are deliberately pending records.

### Phase 2: Define status semantics

Publish and use a controlled status vocabulary:

- Confirmed
- Confirmed from explicit answer
- Inferred from incomplete evidence
- Confirmed but refined
- Provisional
- Superseded
- Engineering decision
- Deferred
- Open
- Rejected

Apply it consistently to D39, D13, D19, D45, and E1–E8.

### Phase 3: Preserve complete provenance in the repository

For each decision, preserve:

- exact question,
- answer,
- timestamp,
- session/thread ID,
- source message identifier,
- transcript hash,
- any quota/error event,
- interpretation notes.

The current `~/.codex` and `C:\Users\User\.gstack` paths should be replaced or supplemented with repository-local archival artifacts or stable external references. The truncated `...01a07ff3-...jsonl` reference must be resolved to a complete identifier.

The continued Q40–Q45 session requires its own source reference.

D39 should retain the quota interruption as an explicit evidence limitation.

### Phase 4: Restore proposal and alternative history

Reconstruct P1–P9 as full records, not just a disposition table. For each proposal preserve:

- proposal text,
- problem being solved,
- alternatives,
- recommendation,
- decision outcome,
- rationale,
- related PRD clauses,
- resulting E# or D#.

This is necessary to preserve findings and obstacles, not just final outcomes.

### Phase 5: Add a canonical glossary and alias map

Define authoritative terms:

- Agent, not Expert
- Pack
- Operation definition
- Operation workspace/chat
- Operation task/run
- Workspace
- Container
- Skill
- Tool
- Connector
- central control-plane PostgreSQL
- container-local SQLite
- platform credits
- billing/metering

Mark all D29–D43 “Expert” wording as historical terminology superseded by D44. Keep the historical text for provenance, but prevent it from being interpreted as current naming guidance.

### Phase 6: Build a PRD reconciliation matrix

For every affected PRD section, record:

- PRD section and clause,
- current PRD text,
- authoritative decision,
- conflict type,
- current status,
- required action.

At minimum include:

- §4.1–§4.2: Agents/Operations taxonomy
- §5: six-Agent pack and reusable identities
- §6: gateway, SQLite, and process lifecycle
- §8: runtime task detection and grant policy
- §9: provider/credit model
- §11: memory scopes and publication
- §17: D23–D25 architecture decisions
- §19: criteria 22–27
- §21: navigation/design dispositions
- §22: resolved versus open engineering TODOs
- §24: decision synchronization rule

The matrix should clearly identify whether each PRD clause is:

- still authoritative,
- superseded,
- refined,
- historical,
- pending PRD update.

### Phase 7: Add unresolved and delegated-work registers

Separate unresolved questions from deferred future work. Each item should include:

- question,
- why it matters,
- affected decisions,
- owner,
- decision deadline,
- default currently used,
- implementation blocker,
- required evidence.

This should include the credential, credits, admin, event retention, workspace, pack schema, Operation mapping, coordinator, and recovery issues identified above.

### Phase 8: Add traceability to implementation and verification

Map each D/E decision and each acceptance criterion to:

- contract/schema,
- runtime or web module,
- persistence model,
- security policy,
- automated test,
- manual QA scenario,
- runbook or operational procedure,
- evidence artifact.

D45 criteria 22–27 must be included in the canonical definition of done, not left only in the decision narrative.

### Phase 9: Add automated documentation checks

CI should validate:

- contiguous and unique decision IDs,
- no README range mismatch,
- all supersession references resolve,
- all PRD section references exist,
- all relative Markdown links resolve,
- all decision statuses use the controlled vocabulary,
- every confirmed decision has provenance,
- every proposal has a disposition,
- every acceptance criterion has an owner and test/evidence reference,
- canonical terminology is not reintroduced in new normative documents,
- PRD synchronization status is explicit.

## Priority order

1. Correct the D40–D49 numbering error.
2. Resolve the D39 and D45 status contradictions.
3. Add the canonical Agent/Expert glossary and mark historical terminology.
4. Create the PRD reconciliation matrix for §§4–6, 8–9, 11, 17, 19, 21–24.
5. Preserve continued-session provenance for D40–D45.
6. Restore full P1–P9 proposal content.
7. Add unresolved and acceptance-traceability registers.
8. Automate consistency checks in CI.

The existing records are a strong decision capture draft, but until these steps are completed, an implementer can still follow the obsolete three-agent/task-chat PRD model, misunderstand which decisions are truly confirmed, or lose the rationale and evidence behind the final architecture.</result>

9	## D1 — Container is the prototype isolation boundary, not a permanent data architecture
10	**Status:** Confirmed
11	**Decision:** Version one runs one isolated container per user. The container is the isolation boundary, but it must not become an architectural trap: central PostgreSQL may later own conversations, tasks, memories, or provider configuration. No feature may assume "all state lives on the container filesystem."

15	**Status:** Confirmed (refined)

17	- **Central PostgreSQL holds only control-plane records:** account + Google OAuth identity, signup cap and administrator flags, user→container mapping, container tier/provisioning state/health/resource summary, container placement and runtime endpoint metadata, backup/snapshot metadata and restore operations, operational security events required by the control plane, emergency commands (restart/stop/cancel-all).
18	- **Container-local database holds product state:** conversations and messages, task and group-chat state, agent execution state, approvals and permission decisions, event history and replay cursors, memories and memory provenance, schedules and event watchers, tool inventory and tool versions, provider configuration metadata, recovery checkpoints, Git/worktree state, usage and execution history.

20	- **Migration readiness:** domain records get stable IDs and repository interfaces now; no domain code is coupled directly to filesystem or database implementation.

16	## D41 — Chat taxonomy: Experts + Operations only, with ad-hoc Operations
17	**Status:** Confirmed — Q41 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 1")
18	**Decision:** The sidebar has exactly two chat sections: **Experts** (persistent direct chats) and **Operations** (collaborative chats). The PRD's user-created "task-specific chat" type is retired as a separate type (PRD itself unchanged; recorded here per PRD §24).
19	- A direct expert chat handles single-expert work and spawns task runs automatically when execution requires lifecycle supervision (D34).
20	- All multi-expert work happens inside an Operation. An Operation instance is either **pack-defined** (instantiated from the pack's `operation.yaml`) or **ad-hoc** (the user selects a custom expert roster from the same pack on the spot, with no reusable definition behind it).
21	- Ad-hoc Operations obey the same rules as pack-defined ones: same-pack roster, runtime-validated roster changes (D36), and full pinning of pack version, roster, skills/tools/connectors, and provider snapshot (D29).
22	- This supersedes the group-chat lifecycle in PRD §4.2; Operation chats remain reusable workspaces with independently tracked task runs (D31).
23	
24	## D42 — Operation coordinator
25	**Status:** Confirmed — Q42 answered 2026-09-11 ("ok", recommendation accepted)
26	**Decision:** The General Assistant is the **default coordinator** of every Operation, but it is removable.
27	- Pack definitions may name a different coordinator expert (`coordinator: <expert-id>`) or `coordinator: none`.
28	- The user may remove the GA from any Operation, including pack-defined ones.
29	- Without a coordinator, experts coordinate peer-to-peer through the typed collaboration protocol (D7). The runtime remains the safety and lifecycle authority in all cases (D4) — removing the coordinator changes who synthesizes, never who validates.
30	- Coordinator proposals for roster changes still follow D36 (user controls; runtime validates).
31	
32	## D43 — Student pack v1 scope
33	**Status:** Confirmed — Q43 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 2")
34	**Decision:** The first pack (`agent-packs/student/v1`) ships with **6 experts** and **3 pack-defined Operations**:
35	- **Experts:** General Assistant, Researcher, Developer, Citation Manager, Programming Tutor, Study Planner.
36	- **Operations:** Research Paper, Programming Assignment, Exam Preparation.
37	- Each expert is reused across at least two Operations (e.g., Researcher in Research Paper and Exam Preparation) so D39's scoped-assignment reuse model is genuinely exercised.
38	- Ad-hoc Operations (D41) cover any combination outside these three.
39	- Adding more experts/Operations later is pack content work (D18), not runtime work; the ~10-expert catalog sketched in the session remains a future content addition.
40	
41	## D44 — Terminology: "Agent" everywhere; Operation framing retained
42	**Status:** Confirmed — Q44 answered 2026-09-11 ("Agent everywhere, option 1. dont drop operation framing. Operation -> Agents.")
43	**Decision:** The product vocabulary is **Pack → Operations → Agents**. The word "Agent" is used everywhere — UI copy, product docs, code identifiers, Zod schemas, DB tables, and pack YAML. The session's word "Expert" is retired.
44	- The Operations framing is unchanged in substance; only the noun changes: an Operation assembles **Agents** (formerly "Experts"), and the sidebar sections are **Agents** and **Operations**.
45	- Decisions 29–43 say "Expert"; read "Expert" as "Agent". Their substance is unchanged and the historical wording in the records is preserved as-is.
46	- One-to-one mapping: Expert (in earlier decisions) = Agent. There is no separate "Expert" concept.
47	
48	## D45 — Definition of done: six additional acceptance criteria
49	**Status:** Confirmed — Q45 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 1")
50	**Decision:** The prototype's definition of done adds six acceptance criteria to PRD §19 (recorded here; the PRD itself remains unchanged):
51	22. A workspace is pinned to a versioned customer pack, and existing tasks retain the pack/Operation/agent versions that created them (D29, D40).
52	23. An Operation launches through a preview that shows agents, skills, connectors, provider/model, estimated credits, and approval requirements before execution (D30).
53	24. One Operation chat can hold multiple independently tracked task runs without merging their state, events, or cost (D31, D34).
54	25. A long-running task survives browser closure, keeps its worker alive while healthy, and resumes from a semantic checkpoint after a restart (D32, D33).
55	26. Read-only tools run automatically, local mutations show a preview, and a matching reusable grant executes without another prompt while still being policy-checked (D35).
56	27. Agent-private memory does not leak into Operation context without explicit publication (D37).
57

59	
60	# Resolution of draft proposals
61	
62	The frontier is now empty. Disposition of every pre-written draft: P1 was decided by D44; P2/P4/P7 were already decided by the PRD; the rest are engineering-level choices that PRD §24 explicitly delegates to engineering, recorded here as E1–E8 so nothing stays silently assumed.
63	
64	| Draft | Disposition |
65	|---|---|
66	| P1 Terminology | **Resolved by D44** — "Agent" everywhere; Operation framing retained. |
67	| P2 Concurrency and admission | **Already decided by PRD §6** (parallelism within limits; running work continues, new jobs queue). Refinement: E1. |
68	| P3 Event retention | **Engineering decision E2.** |
69	| P4 Approval reminder ownership | **Already decided by PRD §8** (reminder times, muting, task stays paused). Refinement: E3. |
70	| P5 Agent-created tool lifecycle | **Already decided by PRD §7** (non-destructive auto-activation, approval for risky, inventory scope). Refinement: E4. |
71	| P6 Scheduler/watcher semantics | **Already decided by PRD §8/§13** (triggers, no duplication on restart). Refinement: E5. |
72	| P7 Git/merge coordination | **Already decided by PRD §10** (branches/worktrees, conflict queueing, merge approval). Refinement: E6. |
73	| P8 Build order and definition of done | **Engineering decision E7**, anchored on D23 Phase 0–7 and D45. |
74	| P9 Testing default | **Engineering decision E8**, anchored on D23's testing strategy. |
75	
76	## E1 — Concurrency and admission control
77	The runtime enforces CPU, memory, process-count, disk, and provider-concurrency limits per container. When limits are reached, new work queues; healthy running work and approval-waiting work are never killed for capacity. Only an explicit admin emergency action (cancel-all) force-stops work. Idle agent sessions may hibernate immediately under resource pressure (D32).
78	
79	## E2 — Event retention and cursor fallback
80	The event journal is retained for the lifetime of the container. High-volume, low-value events (streamed message deltas) may be compacted under a size policy; conversation content remains durable. If a client cursor falls outside retention, the runtime returns a state snapshot plus a new cursor (extends D26).
81	
82	## E3 — Approval reminder delivery
83	The runtime scheduler owns reminder timing per PRD §8 (immediate, 15 minutes, 45 minutes, 3 hours, 24 hours; none after). Reminders are delivery events, not tasks, and never change task state; muting affects delivery only.
84	
85	## E4 — Agent-created tool lifecycle
86	Agent-created tools are declarative, versioned records in the local tool inventory (name, purpose, input/output schema, owner, scope, risk class, permissions, version). Creation follows the standard preview/approval policy (D35); activation into an agent's or Operation's toolset requires a runtime grant; destructive tools always require explicit approval. Every agent-created tool executes through the runtime executor — no bypass of validation, limits, or approval.
87	
88	## E5 — Scheduler and watcher semantics
89	Schedules and watchers are declarative records evaluated by the runtime scheduler with at-least-once firing, idempotency keys, and de-duplication (D23 Rule 6). Occurrences missed while the container is down are not blindly replayed; the scheduler decides catch-up versus skip by policy and records the decision. Watchers restart after recovery without duplicate firing.
90	
91	## E6 — Git and merge coordination
92	Each coding task gets an isolated branch and worktree; conflicting edits queue. The orchestrator prepares a merge request showing the change summary, changed files, validation status, conflict status, and target branch. Main-branch merges always require explicit user approval; the runtime never force-pushes or silently merges (PRD §10).
93	
94	## E7 — Build order and definition of done
95	Build in D23's Phase 0–7 order. The private prototype is done when PRD §19 criteria 1–21 **plus the six additions adopted in D45** pass for Phase 1–6 scope. Phase 7 (remote/cloud backends, admin console, benchmark trend tracking) is optional for the private prototype.
96	
97	## E8 — Testing default
98	CI runs unit, contract, integration, recovery, and `mock-runtime` E2E suites. `live-provider` tests are opt-in only and never run by default. Deterministic fake provider and fake Pi processes are mandatory `test-kit` components, and the mock path must exercise the real task/runtime code (D23).
99

103	
104	| Earlier | Superseded / refined by | Note |
105	|---|---|---|
106	| D2 initial "split deliberately" | D2 (refined) | Strict local-first: maximum product data in container; central PG = control-plane only. |
107	| D3 long-lived process per role | D32 | Persistent identity + on-demand session + task-governed worker. |
108	| D8 "PostgreSQL as source of truth" | D2, D10 | Product-state authority is container SQLite; central PG is control-plane only. |
109	| D18/D19 three-role agent pack | D29 + D43 | Pack contains an agent catalog, teams, Operations, skills, connectors; student pack v1 = 6 agents, 3 Operations. |
110	| D23 earlier 20-package monorepo | D23 (revised) | Three apps + ≤7 packages + feature modules. |
111	| "Expert" terminology (D29–D43) | D44 | "Agent" everywhere; Pack → Operations → Agents. |
112	| PRD §4.1/§4.2 dashboard and chat types | D29, D30, D31, D41, D44 | Sidebar = Agents + Operations; task-chat type retired; Operation preview; reusable Operation workspaces. |
113	| PRD §5 "three agents" | D29, D39, D43 | Agents become reusable identities inside a customer pack. |

119	From PRD §20 and the grilling session: native mobile/PWA packaging; full browser/desktop takeover; social/X search; production billing and metering; user/admin data export and permanent purge; enterprise orgs/teams/audit UI; multi-region and HA; managed secrets service; connector/OAuth marketplace; tool signing/marketplace/distribution; model routing/fallback optimization at scale; distributed tracing and formal load testing; cross-device sync for local-first data; cloud migration automation; cross-pack agent mixing (requires an explicit bridge policy).

### 232. TOOL RESULT

210	**Trust rules:** a pack is data and configuration, not unrestricted code; prompts cannot bypass authorization; capabilities are upper bounds; tool permissions are rechecked by the runtime; manifests are schema-validated; hashes recorded; changes require compatibility checks; existing tasks remain pinned; packs cannot contain secrets, host paths, Docker socket access, or arbitrary startup commands.
211	
212	## D29 — Revised product model: Pack → Operations → Experts (+ Teams, Skills, Connectors)
213	**Status:** Confirmed (supersedes the agent-only pack framing of D18/D19)
214	**Decision:** The product is hierarchical and navigation is Telegram-like. "Scene" is renamed **Operation**.
215	```text
216	Customer Pack
217	├── Experts            # persistent, reusable agent identities
218	├── Expert Teams       # predefined collaboration rosters
219	├── Operations         # reusable collaborative workspaces/templates
220	├── Skills             # knowledge and workflow instructions
221	├── Connectors         # authenticated external integrations
222	├── onboarding
223	├── defaults
224	└── UI metadata
225	```
226	- **Expert** — a specialized agent with a stable identity, reusable across Operations and also usable in a direct chat.
227	- **Operation** — a task-oriented collaborative workspace/chat that assembles existing experts for a specific kind of work; not a new class of agent and not an independent mini-application.
228	- **Expert Team** — a recommended roster for an Operation; each active expert still runs through the same gateway, task state machine, approval policy, and Pi adapter.
229	- **Skill / Connector** — see D38.
230	
231	The sidebar has two sections: **Experts** (persistent direct chats) and **Operations** (group chats). A direct expert chat can propose creating an Operation; an Operation can also be started from the sidebar. Experts communicate only through the typed collaboration protocol (D7), and the runtime still controls membership, tools, connectors, approvals, leases, credit, artifact access, and Operation state.
232	**Reproducibility:** every task stores `pack_id`, `pack_version`, `pack_hash`, `operation_id`, `operation_version`, `operation_hash`, `expert_roster`, `skill_snapshot`, `connector_snapshot`, `provider_snapshot`.
233	**Terminology:** Pack = audience catalog; Operation = task-oriented collaboration configuration/create workspace; Expert = specialized agent profile; Expert Team = predefined group; Skill = reusable capability/knowledge bundle; Connector = external integration; Task = concrete execution created from an Operation; Workspace = persistent user-owned environment containing tasks, projects, files, and memory.
234	**Product-term note:** "Expert" is the user-facing term; "agent" is the technical term for the same object. Code identifiers use `agent`; UI copy may say "Expert."
235	
236	## D30 — Operation launch uses a preview step
237	**Status:** Confirmed
238	**Decision:** When the user selects an Operation, Shera opens a preview showing the Operation purpose, recommended experts, skills, connectors, provider/model, estimated credit usage, approval requirements, and expected outputs. The user may add or remove experts from the same pack, then confirms before any model task or external action starts. The Operation chat and task are created only after confirmation. An advanced setting may later allow immediate start.
239	
240	## D31 — An Operation chat is a reusable workspace containing multiple independently tracked task runs
241	**Status:** Confirmed
242	**Decision:** Distinguish three objects: **Operation definition** (reusable collaboration template), **Operation workspace/chat** (persistent, reusable, supports follow-ups), and **Operation task/run** (one concrete execution).
243	```text
244	Research Paper Operation
245	└── Research Paper workspace chat
246	    ├── Task: Find sources
247	    ├── Task: Compare theories
248	    ├── Task: Draft literature review
249	    └── Task: Check citations
250	```
251	Each run keeps its own task ID, state machine, event stream, approval history, provider snapshot, credit usage, artifacts, and recovery checkpoint. The chat remains available for follow-ups without merging multiple executions into one untraceable task.
252	
253	## D32 — Three separate lifecycles: persistent expert identity, warm on-demand expert session, persistent task worker
254	**Status:** Confirmed (supersedes D3)
255	**Decision:**
256	1. **Persistent expert identity** — always durable: expert profile, direct chat, private memory, settings, capabilities, Operation memberships, task history. Survives process termination, container restart, pack upgrade, and browser closure.
257	2. **Warm expert session** — a supervised Pi process used for conversation, planning, and coordination. Starts on demand, stays warm while actively used, may hibernate after an idle timeout, is reconstructable from durable state, and is never the sole owner of durable information.
258	3. **Persistent task worker** — a separately supervised process for actual work. If the task is `RUNNING`, `WAITING_FOR_TOOL`, `WAITING_FOR_AGENT`, `WAITING_FOR_APPROVAL`, `RETRYING`, or `CANCELLING`, its lifecycle is governed by the task, not the chat's idle timer.
259	**Rules:** a running worker is never stopped by the expert-session idle timeout; closing the browser does not stop the task; the worker sends heartbeats; the gateway supervises the process tree; progress and checkpoints are written to SQLite; the task lease is renewed while the worker is healthy; a stalled worker is detected separately from a slow but healthy worker; resource pressure may pause or queue new work but should not arbitrarily kill healthy running work; cancellation terminates the worker and its descendants; scheduled Operations may wake the required experts and workers automatically.
260	**Policy:** idle expert with no active task → may hibernate; expert coordinating an active Operation → keep warm when resources permit; healthy long-running task → keep worker alive; worker crash with safe checkpoint → restart and resume; container restart with safe checkpoint → restore and resume; unknown external side effect → pause for review; user cancellation → terminate the complete process tree.
261	**Recovery:** the task itself stays durable. After restart the runtime finds non-terminal tasks, inspects the last checkpoint and last known side effect, decides whether continuation is safe, resumes safe work automatically, pauses work with an uncertain external effect for review, and shows `RECOVERING`, `RESUMED`, or `RECOVERY_REQUIRED`. A task must not claim exact resume unless the tool and step actually support it.
262	
263	## D33 — Semantic checkpoints after meaningful steps, lightweight progress events between
264	**Status:** Confirmed
265	**Decision:** Checkpoint at safe semantic boundaries (search query completed, document downloaded, source set finalized, analysis artifact saved, code patch applied, test command completed, approval received, external request confirmed), not after every token or on an arbitrary timer.
266	**Checkpoint fields:** `task_id`, `step_id`, `checkpoint_version`, `completed_actions`, `pending_action`, `artifact_refs`, `provider_usage`, `lease_state`, `approval_state`, `external_effect_state`, `resume_instructions`, `created_at`.
267	Long-running work continues independently of browser presence; recovery pauses only when an external effect cannot be proven safe to replay.
268	
269	## D34 — Messages and task runs are separate records; the runtime, not the LLM, decides task creation
270	**Status:** Confirmed (refined)
271	**Decision:** Every message is persisted as conversation content; a task run is created only when execution requires lifecycle supervision. Task detection is **entirely outside the agent's reasoning**: the runtime infers it from typed protocol events, never from natural-language classification or an `is_task` field. The agent is not asked to classify messages or carry extra planning burden.
272	```text
273	text response only      → conversation turn
274	tool proposal           → create or attach TaskRun
275	delegation proposal     → create TaskRun
276	approval request        → create or attach TaskRun
277	Operation confirmation  → create TaskRun
278	scheduled trigger       → create TaskRun
279	webhook / file event    → create TaskRun
280	```
281	**Internal objects:** `Conversation`, `Message`, `Turn` (one response cycle), `TaskRun` (work needing lifecycle supervision), `ToolExecution`, `Checkpoint`, `Artifact`. **Pi emits only:** `message_delta`, `tool_proposed`, `delegation_proposed`, `approval_requested`, `progress`, `artifact_published`, `checkpoint`, `completed`, `failed`, `cancelled`. The gateway handles task creation, state transitions, retries, approvals, credit reservation, leases, event journaling, recovery, and cancellation.
282	
283	## D35 — Read-only tools automatic; local mutation previewed; grants bypass prompts; "full access" is explicit scoped grants
284	**Status:** Confirmed (refined)
285	**Decision:**
286	```text
287	Read-only tool                → execute automatically
288	Local file mutation           → show preview and request approval
289	Code execution                → show command, working directory, resource policy, approval state
290	Repository mutation           → show diff/branch/worktree and request approval
291	External write                → request approval
292	Destructive/irreversible      → request explicit approval
293	```
294	A matching reusable grant (`Always approve this tool`, `... for this expert`, `... in this workspace`, `... for this repository/path`) lets the runtime execute without another prompt, but the grant is still checked deterministically for correct user, expert, workspace, tool version, capability, resource scope, operation/task policy, expiry, revocation, and not-broader-than-requested.
295	**"Full access" is not a hidden agent mode** — it is a set of explicit capability grants, visible and revocable in settings. The runtime still enforces tenant, container, path, resource, tool-capability, credential, pack, task-state, provider, and credit policy. A prompt cannot override those rules.
296	
297	## D36 — User controls the expert roster; General Assistant proposes; runtime validates
298	**Status:** Confirmed
299	**Decision:** The user can change the roster directly. The General Assistant may propose `ExpertInvited`, `ExpertRemovalProposed`, `OperationReplanned`, `SkillRequested`, `ConnectorRequested`; the runtime verifies pack membership, Operation policy, permissions, cost, and connector impact before applying. Low-risk additions from the approved expert pool may be auto-accepted; changes that increase cost, access, or external capability require user approval.
300	
301	## D37 — Expert-private memory is private by default; publication into Operation context is explicit
302	**Status:** Confirmed
303	**Decision:**
304	**Scopes:** Account memory, Workspace memory, Operation memory, Task memory, Expert-private memory.
305	| Memory type | Visible to |
306	|---|---|
307	| Expert-private | That expert only |
308	| Direct-chat | That expert and the user |
309	| Operation | User and participating experts |
310	| Task | Task participants and runtime |
311	| Workspace | Agents allowed by workspace policy |
312	| Account | User and explicitly authorized runtime services |
313	An expert may propose `MemoryCandidateProposed` with `memory_id`, `scope`, `summary`, `source_refs`, `confidence`, `sensitivity`, `expires_at`. Policy: public-to-Operation notes may be shared automatically; private memories cannot be copied automatically; sensitive memories require user approval; credentials and secrets are rejected; source references are preserved; the user can inspect, edit, revoke, or forget memory; provenance is retained; pack upgrades do not silently rewrite existing memory.
314	
315	## D38 — Strict separation of Skill, Tool, and Connector; effective capability is a layered intersection
316	**Status:** Confirmed
317	**Decision:**
318	- **Skill** = instructions and workflow knowledge (instructions, templates, examples, expected outputs, quality checks, recommended tools, compatible expert roles). A skill cannot execute code, access credentials, or grant permission.
319	- **Tool** = executable capability with a runtime-owned definition: `tool_id`, `version`, `input_schema`, `output_schema`, `capabilities`, `risk_class`, `timeout`, `resource_limits`, `redaction_policy`, `approval_policy`. Experts only propose tool calls; the runtime validates and executes them.
320	- **Connector** = authenticated integration with an external service: authentication configuration, supported actions, tool bindings, permission scopes, connection status, rate limits, redaction rules, account/workspace visibility. An Operation may recommend a connector but cannot automatically authorize or install one.
321	A pack references capabilities rather than duplicating implementations.
322	**Effective capability rule:**
323	```text
324	Platform capability
325	∩ Pack allowance
326	∩ Agent defaults
327	∩ Operation assignment
328	∩ Workspace policy
329	∩ User grants
330	∩ Current task requirements
331	= Effective capability
332	```
333	No lower-level configuration may widen an upper-level restriction (for example, an Operation disabling repository writes overrides a user's general write grant inside that Operation).
334	
335	## D39 — One reusable persistent expert identity with scoped assignments inside each Operation
336	**Status:** Confirmed — recorded from the previously unanswered reply "for the prototype yes"
337	**Decision:** For the prototype, the same expert appearing in multiple Operations has one persistent identity across all Operations. One expert profile, one direct chat, one private memory, and one settings surface. Each Operation gives that expert a scoped assignment.
338	**Rules:** separate Operation context and task history; private memory is not automatically published into Operations (D37); Operation-specific instructions do not permanently rewrite the expert profile; Operation tasks pin the agent and Operation versions used for that task.
339	**Note:** This decision was never written back into the Codex session because the run terminated with `HTTP 402 Payment Required: Budget pool quota has been exhausted` (agentrouter.org) immediately after the user's message.
340

9	# Confirmed in this session
10	
11	## D40 — Workspace model
12	**Status:** Confirmed — Q40 answered 2026-09-11 ("yes go with this", option 1)
13	**Decision:** One workspace per user. The container is the workspace; there is no workspace switcher in the UI. All product records are still scoped by `workspace_id` internally, so multi-workspace can be enabled later without a data migration. The workspace owns tasks, projects, files, memories, and its expert/Operation configuration, and is pinned to the assigned customer pack.
14	**Rationale:** matches one-container-per-user (D1/D2) and the simplicity goal (D16) while preserving the multi-workspace option that D19 assumed.
15	
16	## D41 — Chat taxonomy: Experts + Operations only, with ad-hoc Operations
17	**Status:** Confirmed — Q41 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 1")
18	**Decision:** The sidebar has exactly two chat sections: **Experts** (persistent direct chats) and **Operations** (collaborative chats). The PRD's user-created "task-specific chat" type is retired as a separate type (PRD itself unchanged; recorded here per PRD §24).
19	- A direct expert chat handles single-expert work and spawns task runs automatically when execution requires lifecycle supervision (D34).
20	- All multi-expert work happens inside an Operation. An Operation instance is either **pack-defined** (instantiated from the pack's `operation.yaml`) or **ad-hoc** (the user selects a custom expert roster from the same pack on the spot, with no reusable definition behind it).
21	- Ad-hoc Operations obey the same rules as pack-defined ones: same-pack roster, runtime-validated roster changes (D36), and full pinning of pack version, roster, skills/tools/connectors, and provider snapshot (D29).
22	- This supersedes the group-chat lifecycle in PRD §4.2; Operation chats remain reusable workspaces with independently tracked task runs (D31).
23	
24	## D42 — Operation coordinator
25	**Status:** Confirmed — Q42 answered 2026-09-11 ("ok", recommendation accepted)
26	**Decision:** The General Assistant is the **default coordinator** of every Operation, but it is removable.
27	- Pack definitions may name a different coordinator expert (`coordinator: <expert-id>`) or `coordinator: none`.
28	- The user may remove the GA from any Operation, including pack-defined ones.
29	- Without a coordinator, experts coordinate peer-to-peer through the typed collaboration protocol (D7). The runtime remains the safety and lifecycle authority in all cases (D4) — removing the coordinator changes who synthesizes, never who validates.
30	- Coordinator proposals for roster changes still follow D36 (user controls; runtime validates).
31	
32	## D43 — Student pack v1 scope
33	**Status:** Confirmed — Q43 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 2")
34	**Decision:** The first pack (`agent-packs/student/v1`) ships with **6 experts** and **3 pack-defined Operations**:
35	- **Experts:** General Assistant, Researcher, Developer, Citation Manager, Programming Tutor, Study Planner.
36	- **Operations:** Research Paper, Programming Assignment, Exam Preparation.
37	- Each expert is reused across at least two Operations (e.g., Researcher in Research Paper and Exam Preparation) so D39's scoped-assignment reuse model is genuinely exercised.
38	- Ad-hoc Operations (D41) cover any combination outside these three.
39	- Adding more experts/Operations later is pack content work (D18), not runtime work; the ~10-expert catalog sketched in the session remains a future content addition.
40	
41	## D44 — Terminology: "Agent" everywhere; Operation framing retained
42	**Status:** Confirmed — Q44 answered 2026-09-11 ("Agent everywhere, option 1. dont drop operation framing. Operation -> Agents.")
43	**Decision:** The product vocabulary is **Pack → Operations → Agents**. The word "Agent" is used everywhere — UI copy, product docs, code identifiers, Zod schemas, DB tables, and pack YAML. The session's word "Expert" is retired.
44	- The Operations framing is unchanged in substance; only the noun changes: an Operation assembles **Agents** (formerly "Experts"), and the sidebar sections are **Agents** and **Operations**.
45	- Decisions 29–43 say "Expert"; read "Expert" as "Agent". Their substance is unchanged and the historical wording in the records is preserved as-is.
46	- One-to-one mapping: Expert (in earlier decisions) = Agent. There is no separate "Expert" concept.
47	
48	## D45 — Definition of done: six additional acceptance criteria
49	**Status:** Confirmed — Q45 answered 2026-09-11 ("option 1")
50	**Decision:** The prototype's definition of done adds six acceptance criteria to PRD §19 (recorded here; the PRD itself remains unchanged):
51	22. A workspace is pinned to a versioned customer pack, and existing tasks retain the pack/Operation/agent versions that created them (D29, D40).
52	23. An Operation launches through a preview that shows agents, skills, connectors, provider/model, estimated credits, and approval requirements before execution (D30).
53	24. One Operation chat can hold multiple independently tracked task runs without merging their state, events, or cost (D31, D34).
54	25. A long-running task survives browser closure, keeps its worker alive while healthy, and resumes from a semantic checkpoint after a restart (D32, D33).
55	26. Read-only tools run automatically, local mutations show a preview, and a matching reusable grant executes without another prompt while still being policy-checked (D35).
56	27. Agent-private memory does not leak into Operation context without explicit publication (D37).

62	The frontier is now empty. Disposition of every pre-written draft: P1 was decided by D44; P2/P4/P7 were already decided by the PRD; the rest are engineering-level choices that PRD §24 explicitly delegates to engineering, recorded here as E1–E8 so nothing stays silently assumed.

65	|---|---|
66	| P1 Terminology | **Resolved by D44** — "Agent" everywhere; Operation framing retained. |
67	| P2 Concurrency and admission | **Already decided by PRD §6** (parallelism within limits; running work continues, new jobs queue). Refinement: E1. |
68	| P3 Event retention | **Engineering decision E2.** |

71	| P6 Scheduler/watcher semantics | **Already decided by PRD §8/§13** (triggers, no duplication on restart). Refinement: E5. |
72	| P7 Git/merge coordination | **Already decided by PRD §10** (branches/worktrees, conflict queueing, merge approval). Refinement: E6. |
73	| P8 Build order and definition of done | **Engineering decision E7**, anchored on D23 Phase 0–7 and D45. |
74	| P9 Testing default | **Engineering decision E8**, anchored on D23's testing strategy. |
75	
76	## E1 — Concurrency and admission control
77	The runtime enforces CPU, memory, process-count, disk, and provider-concurrency limits per container. When limits are reached, new work queues; healthy running work and approval-waiting work are never killed for capacity. Only an explicit admin emergency action (cancel-all) force-stops work. Idle agent sessions may hibernate immediately under resource pressure (D32).
78	
79	## E2 — Event retention and cursor fallback
80	The event journal is retained for the lifetime of the container. High-volume, low-value events (streamed message deltas) may be compacted under a size policy; conversation content remains durable. If a client cursor falls outside retention, the runtime returns a state snapshot plus a new cursor (extends D26).
81	
82	## E3 — Approval reminder delivery
83	The runtime scheduler owns reminder timing per PRD §8 (immediate, 15 minutes, 45 minutes, 3 hours, 24 hours; none after). Reminders are delivery events, not tasks, and never change task state; muting affects delivery only.
84	
85	## E4 — Agent-created tool lifecycle
86	Agent-created tools are declarative, versioned records in the local tool inventory (name, purpose, input/output schema, owner, scope, risk class, permissions, version). Creation follows the standard preview/approval policy (D35); activation into an agent's or Operation's toolset requires a runtime grant; destructive tools always require explicit approval. Every agent-created tool executes through the runtime executor — no bypass of validation, limits, or approval.
87	
88	## E5 — Scheduler and watcher semantics
89	Schedules and watchers are declarative records evaluated by the runtime scheduler with at-least-once firing, idempotency keys, and de-duplication (D23 Rule 6). Occurrences missed while the container is down are not blindly replayed; the scheduler decides catch-up versus skip by policy and records the decision. Watchers restart after recovery without duplicate firing.
90	
91	## E6 — Git and merge coordination
92	Each coding task gets an isolated branch and worktree; conflicting edits queue. The orchestrator prepares a merge request showing the change summary, changed files, validation status, conflict status, and target branch. Main-branch merges always require explicit user approval; the runtime never force-pushes or silently merges (PRD §10).
93	
94	## E7 — Build order and definition of done
95	Build in D23's Phase 0–7 order. The private prototype is done when PRD §19 criteria 1–21 **plus the six additions adopted in D45** pass for Phase 1–6 scope. Phase 7 (remote/cloud backends, admin console, benchmark trend tracking) is optional for the private prototype.
96	
97	## E8 — Testing default
98	CI runs unit, contract, integration, recovery, and `mock-runtime` E2E suites. `live-provider` tests are opt-in only and never run by default. Deterministic fake provider and fake Pi processes are mandatory `test-kit` components, and the mock path must exercise the real task/runtime code (D23).
99

112	| PRD §4.1/§4.2 dashboard and chat types | D29, D30, D31, D41, D44 | Sidebar = Agents + Operations; task-chat type retired; Operation preview; reusable Operation workspaces. |

9	## D1 — Container is the prototype isolation boundary, not a permanent data architecture
10	**Status:** Confirmed
11	**Decision:** Version one runs one isolated container per user. The container is the isolation boundary, but it must not become an architectural trap: central PostgreSQL may later own conversations, tasks, memories, or provider configuration. No feature may assume "all state lives on the container filesystem."
12	**Implication:** Define storage ports and stable IDs from the beginning (see D2).
